=== chapter p5-terrain
title: Terrain
blurb: Heightmaps as flat arrays, fBm and diamond-square terrain, island masks, terraces, thermal and droplet hydraulic erosion, normals and hillshading, a terrain mesh exported as OBJ, and a Voxel Space renderer.
--- body
## Terrain is a function z = h(x, y)

Most game terrain is a **heightfield**: one height per point of a regular grid. It cannot represent overhangs or caves (those need voxels or SDFs, see the ray marching chapter), but it is compact, trivially collidable, easy to mesh, easy to stream in tiles, and every terrain editor and engine (Unity, Unreal, Godot, World Machine, Gaea) is built around it.

In C a heightmap is a flat array of `float`s, row-major, exactly like the image buffers from the first chapter of this track:

```c
typedef struct { int w, h; float *z; } Heightmap;    /* height of (x, y) is z[y * w + x] */
```

> **Performance:** one allocation for the whole map, never a `float **` array of rows (one allocation per row, rows scattered in memory, an extra pointer load per access). Walk it with `y` in the outer loop and `x` in the inner loop so you touch memory in order; a 1025×1025 map is 4 MB, bigger than most L2 caches, and walking it column by column misses the cache on almost every access. Compute `(size_t)y * w + x` in `size_t`: `int` multiplication overflows at 46341×46341, which tiled worlds reach.

Conventions for this whole chapter: `x` grows to the right, `y` grows down the image (and "north to south" in the world), `z` is up, heights live roughly in `[0, 1]` and are multiplied by a *height scale* when they become geometry.

## fBm terrain

The previous chapters built gradient noise and **fractal Brownian motion**: sum octaves of noise, each with the frequency multiplied by the *lacunarity* (usually 2) and the amplitude by the *gain* (usually 0.5). Musgrave named and analyzed this in *Texturing and Modeling: A Procedural Approach* (Ebert, Musgrave, Peachey, Perlin, Worley); a gain of 0.5 gives a spectrum whose amplitude falls off like `1/f`, which is roughly what real landscapes measure. Large octaves make continents and mountain ranges, small ones make bumps.

Pure fBm looks like rolling hills, statistically the same everywhere. Real terrain is not: valleys are smooth, peaks are rough. Musgrave's fixes are worth knowing by name:

- **Ridged multifractal**: use `1 - |noise|` per octave, squared. The zero crossings of noise become sharp ridges. Each octave is also weighted by the previous octave's value, so detail accumulates on the ridges and valleys stay smooth.
- **Heterogeneous terrain** (hybrid multifractal): scale each octave by the current height, for the same "rough high, smooth low" effect.
- **Domain warping** (Quilez): sample `fbm(p + k * fbm2(p))`; it bends ridges into convincing folded and eroded shapes for the cost of extra noise calls.
- **Redistribution**: `pow(h, e)` with `e > 1` flattens lowlands into valleys and keeps peaks sharp; Red Blob Games' "Making maps with noise functions" (Amit Patel) is the best short tour of these tricks.

A complete program (the site draws the `P2` image it prints):

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <math.h>

static uint32_t hash2(int x, int y, uint32_t s) {
    uint32_t h = (uint32_t)x * 0x27d4eb2dU ^ (uint32_t)y * 0x165667b1U ^ s;
    h ^= h >> 15; h *= 0x2c1b3c6dU; h ^= h >> 12; h *= 0x297a2d39U; h ^= h >> 15;
    return h;
}
static float vnoise(float x, float y, uint32_t s) {      /* value noise in [0, 1] */
    float x0 = floorf(x), y0 = floorf(y);
    int ix = (int)x0, iy = (int)y0;
    float fx = x - x0, fy = y - y0;
    fx = fx * fx * (3 - 2 * fx); fy = fy * fy * (3 - 2 * fy);
    float a = hash2(ix, iy, s) / 4294967296.0f, b = hash2(ix + 1, iy, s) / 4294967296.0f;
    float c = hash2(ix, iy + 1, s) / 4294967296.0f, d = hash2(ix + 1, iy + 1, s) / 4294967296.0f;
    return a + (b - a) * fx + (c - a) * fy + (a - b - c + d) * fx * fy;
}
static float ridged(float x, float y, int octaves) {    /* Musgrave-style ridged multifractal */
    float sum = 0, amp = 0.5f, weight = 1, norm = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
        float n = 1 - fabsf(vnoise(x, y, 77u + i) * 2 - 1);  /* ridge where noise crosses 0.5 */
        n = n * n * weight;
        weight = fminf(1, n * 2);                         /* detail follows the ridges */
        sum += n * amp; norm += amp;
        x *= 2.03f; y *= 2.03f; amp *= 0.5f;
    }
    return sum / norm;
}

int main(void) {
    enum { W = 128, H = 128 };
    printf("P2\n%d %d\n255\n", W, H);   /* left: fBm^2, right: ridged */
    for (int y = 0; y < H; y++) {
        for (int x = 0; x < W / 2; x++) {
            float fx = x / 24.0f, fy = y / 24.0f, sum = 0, amp = 0.5f, norm = 0;
            for (int o = 0; o < 6; o++) {
                sum += amp * vnoise(fx, fy, 1u + o); norm += amp;
                fx *= 2; fy *= 2; amp *= 0.5f;
            }
            float h = sum / norm;
            printf("%d ", (int)(h * h * 255));             /* redistribution: h^2 */
        }
        for (int x = 0; x < W / 2; x++)
            printf("%d ", (int)(ridged(x / 32.0f, y / 32.0f, 6) * 255));
        printf("\n");
    }
    return 0;
}
```

## Midpoint displacement and diamond-square

Before noise functions were cheap, Fournier, Fussell and Carpenter (1982) generated terrain by **midpoint displacement**: start with the corners, set each midpoint to the average of its endpoints plus a random offset, halve the scale, repeat. Gavin Miller's **diamond-square** (1986) fixes the creases of the naive 2D version by alternating two passes on a `(2^n + 1)²` grid:

```text
diamond step: center of each square = average of its 4 corners   + random * amp
square step : middle of each edge   = average of its 4 neighbors + random * amp   (3 at the border)
then: step /= 2, amp *= roughness
```

The `roughness` factor plays the role of fBm's gain: 0.5 gives the `1/f` look, higher is craggier. Diamond-square is fast (every point is written exactly once, O(n)), but it is a whole-map algorithm: you cannot ask "what is the height at (x, y)?" without generating the map, and tiles do not join. That is why streaming worlds use noise.

> **Performance:** where the random offsets come from matters. Calling a sequential PRNG in loop order makes the map depend on the *visiting order*: change the loop and the world changes, and you cannot generate it in parallel. Hash the point's coordinates with the seed instead (`hash01(x, y, seed)`): every point gets the same value regardless of order, the map is reproducible, and threads or GPU invocations can fill it in any order.

## Shaping: islands and terraces

Noise has no idea it should be an island. A **falloff mask** fixes that: compute each point's distance `d` from the map center (normalized so the edge midpoints are at 1) and multiply the height by `1 - smoothstep(inner, outer, d)`. Using `max(|nx|, |ny|)` instead of the Euclidean distance gives square continents; adding noise to `d` gives ragged coasts.

**Terracing** quantizes height into steps, the rice-paddy and mesa look: `t = h * levels`, keep the integer part, and push the fraction through a curve (`pow(f, sharpness)`) so each level is mostly flat with a steep riser. It is a pure per-point function, so it costs one pass.

## Erosion

Noise terrain looks fake mostly because it is not eroded: real mountains have talus slopes, drainage channels and flat sediment-filled valleys. Musgrave, Kolb and Mace ("The synthesis and rendering of eroded fractal terrains", SIGGRAPH 1989) introduced the two classic simulations.

**Thermal erosion** models rock crumbling off slopes steeper than the *talus angle*. For each cell, look at the neighbors lower by more than the threshold `T`, and move material proportionally to them:

```text
d_i     = h - h_i            for each neighbor i
d_max   = max d_i,   d_total = sum of d_i where d_i > T
move to neighbor i = rate * (d_max - T) * d_i / d_total      (for d_i > T)
```

(This is Olsen's formulation, "Realtime Procedural Terrain Generation", 2004.) The important implementation detail: **accumulate the changes in a separate buffer and apply them after the pass**. Updating in place makes the result depend on scan order: material cascades along the scan direction in one pass and the terrain grows a directional grain.

**Hydraulic erosion** simulates water. Grid-based versions (water and sediment fields per cell, Mei et al. 2007) map nicely to GPUs; the **droplet** (particle) method, from Hans Theobald Beyer's 2015 thesis and popularized by Sebastian Lague's *Coding Adventure: Hydraulic Erosion*, is simpler and looks great on the CPU. Each droplet:

1. samples the height and gradient at its position (bilinear interpolation of the 4 surrounding grid points),
2. updates its direction: `dir = dir * inertia - gradient * (1 - inertia)`, normalized, and moves one cell along it,
3. computes the height difference `dh` between new and old position and its sediment **capacity** `max(-dh * speed * water * capacity_factor, min_capacity)`: fast, downhill, voluminous water carries more,
4. **deposits** if it carries more than its capacity or went uphill (filling the pit), otherwise **erodes** a fraction of the spare capacity (never more than `-dh`, so it does not dig holes),
5. speeds up going downhill (`speed^2 - dh * gravity`), and evaporates a little.

Tens of thousands of droplets carve gullies, smooth valleys and leave alluvial fans. Because every droplet's start comes from `hash(index, seed)`, the result is deterministic.

> **Performance:** a droplet does about 30 steps of a few multiplies; 100,000 droplets is 3 million steps, fine on a CPU *if the inner loop allocates nothing*. Precompute anything shared (Lague precomputes his erosion brush weights once, not per step), keep the map a flat `float` array, and keep droplet state in local variables (registers), not in a heap-allocated struct per droplet.

## Normals and shading

The surface `z = s * h(x, y)` (with height scale `s`) has the (unnormalized) normal `(-s * dh/dx, -s * dh/dy, 1)`. Estimate the derivatives with **central differences**: `dh/dx ≈ (h(x+1, y) - h(x-1, y)) / 2`. At the border, clamp the neighbor coordinate to the grid. Normals turn a heightmap into something readable:

- **Hillshade** (the GIS classic, after Horn 1981): brightness `= ambient + diffuse * max(0, n · L)` for a light direction `L` from an *azimuth* and an *altitude*. Cartographers light from the north-west.
- **Slope** `1 - n.z` (0 flat, near 1 for cliffs) picks rock vs grass; **height** picks water, sand, snow. A few thresholds and lerps go a long way.

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <math.h>

static uint32_t hash2(int x, int y, uint32_t s) {
    uint32_t h = (uint32_t)x * 0x27d4eb2dU ^ (uint32_t)y * 0x165667b1U ^ s;
    h ^= h >> 15; h *= 0x2c1b3c6dU; h ^= h >> 12; h *= 0x297a2d39U; h ^= h >> 15;
    return h;
}
static float vnoise(float x, float y, uint32_t s) {
    float x0 = floorf(x), y0 = floorf(y);
    int ix = (int)x0, iy = (int)y0;
    float fx = x - x0, fy = y - y0;
    fx = fx * fx * (3 - 2 * fx); fy = fy * fy * (3 - 2 * fy);
    float a = hash2(ix, iy, s) / 4294967296.0f, b = hash2(ix + 1, iy, s) / 4294967296.0f;
    float c = hash2(ix, iy + 1, s) / 4294967296.0f, d = hash2(ix + 1, iy + 1, s) / 4294967296.0f;
    return a + (b - a) * fx + (c - a) * fy + (a - b - c + d) * fx * fy;
}

enum { N = 128 };
static float hm[N * N];

static float at(int x, int y) {                     /* clamped lookup */
    x = x < 0 ? 0 : x >= N ? N - 1 : x;
    y = y < 0 ? 0 : y >= N ? N - 1 : y;
    return hm[y * N + x];
}

int main(void) {
    for (int y = 0; y < N; y++)                     /* fBm with an island mask */
        for (int x = 0; x < N; x++) {
            float fx = x / 40.0f, fy = y / 40.0f, sum = 0, amp = 0.5f;
            for (int o = 0; o < 6; o++) { sum += amp * vnoise(fx, fy, 9u + o); fx *= 2; fy *= 2; amp *= 0.5f; }
            float nx = 2.0f * x / (N - 1) - 1, ny = 2.0f * y / (N - 1) - 1;
            float d = sqrtf(nx * nx + ny * ny), t = fminf(1, fmaxf(0, (d - 0.3f) / 0.65f));
            hm[y * N + x] = sum / 0.984375f * (1 - t * t * (3 - 2 * t));
        }
    const float s = 60, Lx = -0.5f, Ly = -0.5f, Lz = 0.7071f;   /* light from the north-west */
    printf("P3\n%d %d\n255\n", N, N);
    for (int y = 0; y < N; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < N; x++) {
            float dx = (at(x + 1, y) - at(x - 1, y)) * 0.5f * s;
            float dy = (at(x, y + 1) - at(x, y - 1)) * 0.5f * s;
            float il = 1 / sqrtf(dx * dx + dy * dy + 1);
            float nx = -dx * il, ny = -dy * il, nz = il;
            float h = hm[y * N + x], r, g, b;
            if (h < 0.3f) { r = 30; g = 70; b = 150; nx = ny = 0; nz = 1; }
            else if (nz < 0.75f) { r = 120; g = 110; b = 100; }
            else { r = 80 + 100 * h; g = 140; b = 60; }
            float lit = 0.35f + 0.65f * fmaxf(0, nx * Lx + ny * Ly + nz * Lz);
            printf("%d %d %d\n", (int)fminf(255, r * lit), (int)fminf(255, g * lit), (int)fminf(255, b * lit));
        }
    return 0;
}
```

## From heights to a mesh

A grid of `gw × gh` vertices has `(gw - 1) × (gh - 1)` quads, each split into two triangles: `gw * gh` vertices and `6 * (gw - 1) * (gh - 1)` indices. Those counts are known **before** you generate anything, so allocate exactly once:

```c
size_t nverts = (size_t)gw * gh, nidx = (size_t)6 * (gw - 1) * (gh - 1);
float *pos = malloc(nverts * 3 * sizeof *pos);
uint32_t *idx = malloc(nidx * sizeof *idx);
```

An **index buffer** shares each vertex between the up to six triangles that touch it: 4 bytes per index instead of 12+ bytes per repeated vertex, and the GPU's post-transform cache reuses the shaded vertex. Winding order (counter-clockwise seen from above here) decides which side is the front face.

> **Performance:** `realloc` by one element per vertex is the classic mistake: each call may copy the whole array, so building `n` vertices costs O(n²), and under AddressSanitizer every `realloc` copies. Growing by doubling is amortized O(n) and fine when you do not know the size; here you do, so compute it. The same goes for writing text: building a big string with `strcat` rescans everything written so far on every call (O(n²)); keep a write position and `snprintf` at it.

Wavefront **OBJ** is the lingua franca for meshes: lines `v x y z`, `vn x y z`, and faces `f a//na b//nb c//nc` with **1-based** indices. Every DCC tool reads it, which makes it the easiest way to look at your generator's output in Blender.

## Seeing it in 3D: Voxel Space

NovaLogic's *Comanche* (1992) rendered heightmap terrain in real time on a 386 with the **Voxel Space** algorithm (Sebastian Macke's "VoxelSpace" repository is a famous short write-up). For every screen column, walk from the camera outward along that column's ray in the map; at each distance `z`, project the map height to a screen row:

```text
row = (camera_height - height) / z * scale + horizon
```

Walk **front to back** and keep a per-column **y-buffer**, the highest row drawn so far: a sample only draws the part of its column above everything nearer, so occlusion costs one compare and every pixel is written once. Stepping `z` by an increasing `dz` spends fewer samples far away where they would be sub-pixel anyway (level of detail for free). It is an oblique view, not a true 3D projection (no pitch or roll), but it is a striking way to see a heightmap with a few dozen lines of C.

## The project

You will build one terrain generator, step by step, ending with an eroded, shaded island seen from the air:

1. fBm heightmap and normalization
2. diamond-square
3. island mask and terraces
4. thermal erosion
5. bilinear height and gradient sampling
6. one hydraulic erosion droplet
7. many droplets with an erosion brush
8. normals
9. slope and height coloring with hillshade
10. a triangle mesh with sizes computed up front
11. OBJ export without quadratic string building
12. a Voxel Space renderer

The first step's starter contains the helpers from the earlier chapters of this track (vectors, hashing, 2D gradient noise, `fbm2`, the RGB image and its `P3` writer) plus the `Heightmap` type; read it once so you know what is there. Every step's `main` prints an image when you click **Run**.

=== exercise hm_fbm
title: Terrain 1: an fBm heightmap
--- prompt
Fill a heightmap with fBm and normalize it. This is the starting point of the whole chapter's terrain project; the starter contains the helpers from earlier chapters (`hash01`, `gnoise2`, `fbm2`, the `Image` type) and this chapter's `Heightmap`.

Write:

- `void hm_fbm(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float scale, int octaves)`: set the height of every point `(x, y)` to `fbm2(x * scale, y * scale, seed, octaves, 2.0f, 0.5f)`.
- `void hm_minmax(const Heightmap *m, float *lo, float *hi)`: smallest and largest height.
- `void hm_normalize(Heightmap *m)`: remap linearly so the minimum becomes 0 and the maximum 1. If all heights are equal, set them all to 0 (do not divide by zero).

Remember the layout: `m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x]`, `y` in the outer loop.

> **Performance:** in `hm_normalize`, find min and max in one pass, compute `1 / (hi - lo)` once, then multiply in a second pass. A division per element is several times slower than a multiplication; `hi - lo` per element is pure waste.

--- starter
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <math.h>

/* ===== From earlier chapters: vectors, hashing, gradient noise, fBm, images ===== */

typedef struct { float x, y, z; } Vec3;
Vec3 v3(float x, float y, float z) { Vec3 r = {x, y, z}; return r; }
Vec3 v3_add(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x + b.x, a.y + b.y, a.z + b.z); }
Vec3 v3_sub(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y, a.z - b.z); }
Vec3 v3_scale(Vec3 a, float s) { return v3(a.x * s, a.y * s, a.z * s); }
float v3_dot(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return a.x * b.x + a.y * b.y + a.z * b.z; }
Vec3 v3_norm(Vec3 a) { float l = sqrtf(v3_dot(a, a)); return l > 0 ? v3_scale(a, 1.0f / l) : a; }

uint32_t hash32(uint32_t x) {            /* lowbias32 (Chris Wellons) */
    x ^= x >> 16; x *= 0x7feb352dU; x ^= x >> 15; x *= 0x846ca68bU; x ^= x >> 16;
    return x;
}
uint32_t hash2(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {
    return hash32((uint32_t)x * 0x27d4eb2dU ^ hash32((uint32_t)y ^ seed));
}
float hash01(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {     /* uniform in [0, 1) */
    return (float)(hash2(x, y, seed) >> 8) * (1.0f / 16777216.0f);
}
float lerpf(float a, float b, float t) { return a + (b - a) * t; }
float fade(float t) { return t * t * t * (t * (t * 6 - 15) + 10); }   /* Perlin 2002 quintic */
float clampf(float v, float lo, float hi) { return v < lo ? lo : v > hi ? hi : v; }
float smoothstepf(float e0, float e1, float v) {
    float t = clampf((v - e0) / (e1 - e0), 0.0f, 1.0f);
    return t * t * (3 - 2 * t);
}

static const float GRAD_X[8] = {1, -1, 0, 0, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
static const float GRAD_Y[8] = {0, 0, 1, -1, 0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
float grad_dot(uint32_t h, float dx, float dy) { return GRAD_X[h & 7] * dx + GRAD_Y[h & 7] * dy; }

/* 2D gradient (Perlin-style) noise, roughly in [-1, 1], 0 at integer lattice points */
float gnoise2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed) {
    float fx0 = floorf(x), fy0 = floorf(y);
    int ix = (int)fx0, iy = (int)fy0;
    float fx = x - fx0, fy = y - fy0;
    float n00 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy, seed), fx, fy);
    float n10 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy, seed), fx - 1, fy);
    float n01 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy + 1, seed), fx, fy - 1);
    float n11 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy + 1, seed), fx - 1, fy - 1);
    float u = fade(fx), v = fade(fy);
    return 1.41421356f * lerpf(lerpf(n00, n10, u), lerpf(n01, n11, u), v);
}

/* fractal Brownian motion: octaves of noise, frequency x lacunarity, amplitude x gain; result ~[-1, 1] */
float fbm2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed, int octaves, float lacunarity, float gain) {
    float sum = 0, amp = 1, norm = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
        sum += amp * gnoise2(x, y, seed + (uint32_t)i * 7919u);
        norm += amp;
        x *= lacunarity; y *= lacunarity; amp *= gain;
    }
    return norm > 0 ? sum / norm : 0;
}

typedef struct { int w, h; unsigned char *px; } Image;      /* RGB, 3 bytes per pixel, row-major */
Image img_new(int w, int h) { Image im = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h * 3, 1)}; return im; }
void img_free(Image *im) { free(im->px); im->px = NULL; }
void img_set(Image *im, int x, int y, int r, int g, int b) {
    unsigned char *p = im->px + ((size_t)y * im->w + x) * 3;
    p[0] = (unsigned char)r; p[1] = (unsigned char)g; p[2] = (unsigned char)b;
}
void img_write_ppm(const Image *im) {                       /* ASCII P3 to stdout: the site draws it */
    printf("P3\n%d %d\n255\n", im->w, im->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < im->h; y++) {
        const unsigned char *p = im->px + (size_t)y * im->w * 3;
        for (int x = 0; x < im->w * 3; x++) printf("%d%c", p[x], x + 1 < im->w * 3 ? ' ' : '\n');
    }
}

/* ===== This chapter: the heightmap, a flat row-major float array ===== */

typedef struct { int w, h; float *z; } Heightmap;           /* height of (x, y) is z[y * w + x] */
Heightmap hm_new(int w, int h) { Heightmap m = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h, sizeof(float))}; return m; }
void hm_free(Heightmap *m) { free(m->z); m->z = NULL; }
float hm_get(const Heightmap *m, int x, int y) { return m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x]; }
void hm_write_pgm(const Heightmap *m) {                     /* heights in [0, 1] as a P2 image */
    printf("P2\n%d %d\n255\n", m->w, m->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float v = clampf(hm_get(m, x, y), 0, 1);
            printf("%d%c", (int)(v * 255 + 0.5f), x + 1 < m->w ? ' ' : '\n');
        }
}

void hm_fbm(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float scale, int octaves) {
    (void)m; (void)seed; (void)scale; (void)octaves;
}

void hm_minmax(const Heightmap *m, float *lo, float *hi) {
    (void)m;
    *lo = 0; *hi = 0;
}

void hm_normalize(Heightmap *m) {
    (void)m;
}

int main(void) {
    Heightmap m = hm_new(128, 128);
    hm_fbm(&m, 7, 1.0f / 40, 6);
    hm_normalize(&m);
    hm_write_pgm(&m);
    hm_free(&m);
    return 0;
}

--- solution
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <math.h>

/* ===== From earlier chapters: vectors, hashing, gradient noise, fBm, images ===== */

typedef struct { float x, y, z; } Vec3;
Vec3 v3(float x, float y, float z) { Vec3 r = {x, y, z}; return r; }
Vec3 v3_add(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x + b.x, a.y + b.y, a.z + b.z); }
Vec3 v3_sub(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y, a.z - b.z); }
Vec3 v3_scale(Vec3 a, float s) { return v3(a.x * s, a.y * s, a.z * s); }
float v3_dot(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return a.x * b.x + a.y * b.y + a.z * b.z; }
Vec3 v3_norm(Vec3 a) { float l = sqrtf(v3_dot(a, a)); return l > 0 ? v3_scale(a, 1.0f / l) : a; }

uint32_t hash32(uint32_t x) {            /* lowbias32 (Chris Wellons) */
    x ^= x >> 16; x *= 0x7feb352dU; x ^= x >> 15; x *= 0x846ca68bU; x ^= x >> 16;
    return x;
}
uint32_t hash2(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {
    return hash32((uint32_t)x * 0x27d4eb2dU ^ hash32((uint32_t)y ^ seed));
}
float hash01(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {     /* uniform in [0, 1) */
    return (float)(hash2(x, y, seed) >> 8) * (1.0f / 16777216.0f);
}
float lerpf(float a, float b, float t) { return a + (b - a) * t; }
float fade(float t) { return t * t * t * (t * (t * 6 - 15) + 10); }   /* Perlin 2002 quintic */
float clampf(float v, float lo, float hi) { return v < lo ? lo : v > hi ? hi : v; }
float smoothstepf(float e0, float e1, float v) {
    float t = clampf((v - e0) / (e1 - e0), 0.0f, 1.0f);
    return t * t * (3 - 2 * t);
}

static const float GRAD_X[8] = {1, -1, 0, 0, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
static const float GRAD_Y[8] = {0, 0, 1, -1, 0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
float grad_dot(uint32_t h, float dx, float dy) { return GRAD_X[h & 7] * dx + GRAD_Y[h & 7] * dy; }

/* 2D gradient (Perlin-style) noise, roughly in [-1, 1], 0 at integer lattice points */
float gnoise2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed) {
    float fx0 = floorf(x), fy0 = floorf(y);
    int ix = (int)fx0, iy = (int)fy0;
    float fx = x - fx0, fy = y - fy0;
    float n00 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy, seed), fx, fy);
    float n10 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy, seed), fx - 1, fy);
    float n01 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy + 1, seed), fx, fy - 1);
    float n11 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy + 1, seed), fx - 1, fy - 1);
    float u = fade(fx), v = fade(fy);
    return 1.41421356f * lerpf(lerpf(n00, n10, u), lerpf(n01, n11, u), v);
}

/* fractal Brownian motion: octaves of noise, frequency x lacunarity, amplitude x gain; result ~[-1, 1] */
float fbm2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed, int octaves, float lacunarity, float gain) {
    float sum = 0, amp = 1, norm = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
        sum += amp * gnoise2(x, y, seed + (uint32_t)i * 7919u);
        norm += amp;
        x *= lacunarity; y *= lacunarity; amp *= gain;
    }
    return norm > 0 ? sum / norm : 0;
}

typedef struct { int w, h; unsigned char *px; } Image;      /* RGB, 3 bytes per pixel, row-major */
Image img_new(int w, int h) { Image im = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h * 3, 1)}; return im; }
void img_free(Image *im) { free(im->px); im->px = NULL; }
void img_set(Image *im, int x, int y, int r, int g, int b) {
    unsigned char *p = im->px + ((size_t)y * im->w + x) * 3;
    p[0] = (unsigned char)r; p[1] = (unsigned char)g; p[2] = (unsigned char)b;
}
void img_write_ppm(const Image *im) {                       /* ASCII P3 to stdout: the site draws it */
    printf("P3\n%d %d\n255\n", im->w, im->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < im->h; y++) {
        const unsigned char *p = im->px + (size_t)y * im->w * 3;
        for (int x = 0; x < im->w * 3; x++) printf("%d%c", p[x], x + 1 < im->w * 3 ? ' ' : '\n');
    }
}

/* ===== This chapter: the heightmap, a flat row-major float array ===== */

typedef struct { int w, h; float *z; } Heightmap;           /* height of (x, y) is z[y * w + x] */
Heightmap hm_new(int w, int h) { Heightmap m = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h, sizeof(float))}; return m; }
void hm_free(Heightmap *m) { free(m->z); m->z = NULL; }
float hm_get(const Heightmap *m, int x, int y) { return m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x]; }
void hm_write_pgm(const Heightmap *m) {                     /* heights in [0, 1] as a P2 image */
    printf("P2\n%d %d\n255\n", m->w, m->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float v = clampf(hm_get(m, x, y), 0, 1);
            printf("%d%c", (int)(v * 255 + 0.5f), x + 1 < m->w ? ' ' : '\n');
        }
}

void hm_fbm(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float scale, int octaves) {
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++)
            m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x] = fbm2(x * scale, y * scale, seed, octaves, 2.0f, 0.5f);
}

void hm_minmax(const Heightmap *m, float *lo, float *hi) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float a = m->z[0], b = m->z[0];
    for (size_t i = 1; i < n; i++) {
        if (m->z[i] < a) a = m->z[i];
        if (m->z[i] > b) b = m->z[i];
    }
    *lo = a; *hi = b;
}

void hm_normalize(Heightmap *m) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float lo, hi;
    hm_minmax(m, &lo, &hi);
    if (!(hi > lo)) {
        memset(m->z, 0, n * sizeof(float));
        return;
    }
    float s = 1.0f / (hi - lo);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) m->z[i] = (m->z[i] - lo) * s;
}

int main(void) {
    Heightmap m = hm_new(128, 128);
    hm_fbm(&m, 7, 1.0f / 40, 6);
    hm_normalize(&m);
    hm_write_pgm(&m);
    hm_free(&m);
    return 0;
}

--- hint
`fbm2` already exists in the starter; `hm_fbm` is two nested loops around one call.

--- hint
`!(hi > lo)` catches both "all equal" and NaN heights.

--- harness
int main(void) {
    Heightmap m = hm_new(64, 48);
    hm_fbm(&m, 42, 0.05f, 5);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 0, 0) - fbm2(0, 0, 42, 5, 2, 0.5f)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 13, 7) - fbm2(13 * 0.05f, 7 * 0.05f, 42, 5, 2, 0.5f)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 63, 47) - fbm2(63 * 0.05f, 47 * 0.05f, 42, 5, 2, 0.5f)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 50, 3) - fbm2(50 * 0.05f, 3 * 0.05f, 42, 5, 2, 0.5f)) < 1e-6f);
    float lo, hi, rlo = 1e9f, rhi = -1e9f;
    for (int i = 0; i < 64 * 48; i++) { rlo = fminf(rlo, m.z[i]); rhi = fmaxf(rhi, m.z[i]); }
    hm_minmax(&m, &lo, &hi);
    CHECK(lo == rlo && hi == rhi);
    CHECK(lo < 0 && hi > 0);
    float before = hm_get(&m, 20, 30);
    hm_normalize(&m);
    hm_minmax(&m, &lo, &hi);
    CHECK(fabsf(lo) < 1e-6f && fabsf(hi - 1) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 20, 30) - (before - rlo) / (rhi - rlo)) < 1e-5f);
    int below = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 64 * 48; i++) below += m.z[i] < 0 || m.z[i] > 1;
    CHECK_INT(below, 0);
    hm_free(&m);

    Heightmap a = hm_new(32, 32), b = hm_new(32, 32);
    hm_fbm(&a, 1, 0.1f, 4); hm_fbm(&b, 1, 0.1f, 4);
    CHECK(memcmp(a.z, b.z, 32 * 32 * sizeof(float)) == 0);
    hm_fbm(&b, 2, 0.1f, 4);
    CHECK(memcmp(a.z, b.z, 32 * 32 * sizeof(float)) != 0);
    for (int i = 0; i < 32 * 32; i++) a.z[i] = 3.5f;
    hm_normalize(&a);
    int nonzero = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 32 * 32; i++) nonzero += a.z[i] != 0;
    CHECK_INT(nonzero, 0);
    hm_free(&a); hm_free(&b);

    Heightmap one = hm_new(1, 1);
    one.z[0] = -2;
    hm_minmax(&one, &lo, &hi);
    CHECK(lo == -2 && hi == -2);
    hm_free(&one);
    return TEST_END();
}

=== exercise hm_diamond
title: Terrain 2: diamond-square
continues: hm_fbm
--- prompt
Implement Miller's diamond-square algorithm as `int hm_diamond_square(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float roughness)`.

- The map must be square with side `2^k + 1` (2, 3, 5, 9, ..., 1025). Otherwise return 0 and leave the map untouched. Return 1 on success.
- With `n = w - 1`, the four corners `(0,0) (n,0) (0,n) (n,n)` get `hash01(x, y, seed)`.
- Every other point gets a random offset `r(x, y) = (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp`.
- Start with `step = n`, `amp = 0.5f`. While `step > 1`, with `half = step / 2`:
  1. **diamond**: for every square of side `step` (top-left corners at multiples of `step`), its center = average of its 4 corners `+ r`.
  2. **square**: for every edge midpoint (points `(x, y)` on multiples of `half` that are not yet set: exactly one of `x / half`, `y / half` is odd), the value = average of its neighbors at distance `half` left, right, up and down that are **inside the grid** (3 at the border, 4 inside) `+ r`.
  3. `step = half`, `amp *= roughness`.

Do not normalize; the caller can call `hm_normalize`.

Because every random offset comes from hashing the point's own coordinates, the result does not depend on the order you visit points in. (Check for yourself: a square-step point's neighbors are always corners and centers, never other square-step points.)

--- stubs
int hm_diamond_square(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float roughness) {
    (void)m; (void)seed; (void)roughness;
    return 0;
}

--- solution
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <math.h>

/* ===== From earlier chapters: vectors, hashing, gradient noise, fBm, images ===== */

typedef struct { float x, y, z; } Vec3;
Vec3 v3(float x, float y, float z) { Vec3 r = {x, y, z}; return r; }
Vec3 v3_add(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x + b.x, a.y + b.y, a.z + b.z); }
Vec3 v3_sub(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y, a.z - b.z); }
Vec3 v3_scale(Vec3 a, float s) { return v3(a.x * s, a.y * s, a.z * s); }
float v3_dot(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return a.x * b.x + a.y * b.y + a.z * b.z; }
Vec3 v3_norm(Vec3 a) { float l = sqrtf(v3_dot(a, a)); return l > 0 ? v3_scale(a, 1.0f / l) : a; }

uint32_t hash32(uint32_t x) {            /* lowbias32 (Chris Wellons) */
    x ^= x >> 16; x *= 0x7feb352dU; x ^= x >> 15; x *= 0x846ca68bU; x ^= x >> 16;
    return x;
}
uint32_t hash2(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {
    return hash32((uint32_t)x * 0x27d4eb2dU ^ hash32((uint32_t)y ^ seed));
}
float hash01(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {     /* uniform in [0, 1) */
    return (float)(hash2(x, y, seed) >> 8) * (1.0f / 16777216.0f);
}
float lerpf(float a, float b, float t) { return a + (b - a) * t; }
float fade(float t) { return t * t * t * (t * (t * 6 - 15) + 10); }   /* Perlin 2002 quintic */
float clampf(float v, float lo, float hi) { return v < lo ? lo : v > hi ? hi : v; }
float smoothstepf(float e0, float e1, float v) {
    float t = clampf((v - e0) / (e1 - e0), 0.0f, 1.0f);
    return t * t * (3 - 2 * t);
}

static const float GRAD_X[8] = {1, -1, 0, 0, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
static const float GRAD_Y[8] = {0, 0, 1, -1, 0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
float grad_dot(uint32_t h, float dx, float dy) { return GRAD_X[h & 7] * dx + GRAD_Y[h & 7] * dy; }

/* 2D gradient (Perlin-style) noise, roughly in [-1, 1], 0 at integer lattice points */
float gnoise2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed) {
    float fx0 = floorf(x), fy0 = floorf(y);
    int ix = (int)fx0, iy = (int)fy0;
    float fx = x - fx0, fy = y - fy0;
    float n00 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy, seed), fx, fy);
    float n10 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy, seed), fx - 1, fy);
    float n01 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy + 1, seed), fx, fy - 1);
    float n11 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy + 1, seed), fx - 1, fy - 1);
    float u = fade(fx), v = fade(fy);
    return 1.41421356f * lerpf(lerpf(n00, n10, u), lerpf(n01, n11, u), v);
}

/* fractal Brownian motion: octaves of noise, frequency x lacunarity, amplitude x gain; result ~[-1, 1] */
float fbm2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed, int octaves, float lacunarity, float gain) {
    float sum = 0, amp = 1, norm = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
        sum += amp * gnoise2(x, y, seed + (uint32_t)i * 7919u);
        norm += amp;
        x *= lacunarity; y *= lacunarity; amp *= gain;
    }
    return norm > 0 ? sum / norm : 0;
}

typedef struct { int w, h; unsigned char *px; } Image;      /* RGB, 3 bytes per pixel, row-major */
Image img_new(int w, int h) { Image im = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h * 3, 1)}; return im; }
void img_free(Image *im) { free(im->px); im->px = NULL; }
void img_set(Image *im, int x, int y, int r, int g, int b) {
    unsigned char *p = im->px + ((size_t)y * im->w + x) * 3;
    p[0] = (unsigned char)r; p[1] = (unsigned char)g; p[2] = (unsigned char)b;
}
void img_write_ppm(const Image *im) {                       /* ASCII P3 to stdout: the site draws it */
    printf("P3\n%d %d\n255\n", im->w, im->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < im->h; y++) {
        const unsigned char *p = im->px + (size_t)y * im->w * 3;
        for (int x = 0; x < im->w * 3; x++) printf("%d%c", p[x], x + 1 < im->w * 3 ? ' ' : '\n');
    }
}

/* ===== This chapter: the heightmap, a flat row-major float array ===== */

typedef struct { int w, h; float *z; } Heightmap;           /* height of (x, y) is z[y * w + x] */
Heightmap hm_new(int w, int h) { Heightmap m = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h, sizeof(float))}; return m; }
void hm_free(Heightmap *m) { free(m->z); m->z = NULL; }
float hm_get(const Heightmap *m, int x, int y) { return m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x]; }
void hm_write_pgm(const Heightmap *m) {                     /* heights in [0, 1] as a P2 image */
    printf("P2\n%d %d\n255\n", m->w, m->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float v = clampf(hm_get(m, x, y), 0, 1);
            printf("%d%c", (int)(v * 255 + 0.5f), x + 1 < m->w ? ' ' : '\n');
        }
}

void hm_fbm(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float scale, int octaves) {
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++)
            m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x] = fbm2(x * scale, y * scale, seed, octaves, 2.0f, 0.5f);
}

void hm_minmax(const Heightmap *m, float *lo, float *hi) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float a = m->z[0], b = m->z[0];
    for (size_t i = 1; i < n; i++) {
        if (m->z[i] < a) a = m->z[i];
        if (m->z[i] > b) b = m->z[i];
    }
    *lo = a; *hi = b;
}

void hm_normalize(Heightmap *m) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float lo, hi;
    hm_minmax(m, &lo, &hi);
    if (!(hi > lo)) {
        memset(m->z, 0, n * sizeof(float));
        return;
    }
    float s = 1.0f / (hi - lo);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) m->z[i] = (m->z[i] - lo) * s;
}

int hm_diamond_square(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float roughness) {
    int n = m->w - 1, w = m->w;
    if (m->w != m->h || n < 1 || (n & (n - 1)) != 0) return 0;
    float *z = m->z;
    z[0] = hash01(0, 0, seed);
    z[n] = hash01(n, 0, seed);
    z[(size_t)n * w] = hash01(0, n, seed);
    z[(size_t)n * w + n] = hash01(n, n, seed);
    float amp = 0.5f;
    for (int step = n; step > 1; step /= 2, amp *= roughness) {
        int half = step / 2;
        for (int y = half; y < w; y += step)          /* diamond: square centers */
            for (int x = half; x < w; x += step) {
                float avg = (z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x - half] + z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x + half] +
                             z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x - half] + z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x + half]) * 0.25f;
                z[(size_t)y * w + x] = avg + (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp;
            }
        for (int y = 0; y < w; y += half)             /* square: edge midpoints */
            for (int x = (y / half) % 2 == 0 ? half : 0; x < w; x += step) {
                float sum = 0;
                int cnt = 0;
                if (x >= half) { sum += z[(size_t)y * w + x - half]; cnt++; }
                if (x + half < w) { sum += z[(size_t)y * w + x + half]; cnt++; }
                if (y >= half) { sum += z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x]; cnt++; }
                if (y + half < w) { sum += z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x]; cnt++; }
                z[(size_t)y * w + x] = sum / cnt + (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp;
            }
    }
    return 1;
}

int main(void) {
    Heightmap m = hm_new(129, 129);
    hm_diamond_square(&m, 2024, 0.55f);
    hm_normalize(&m);
    Heightmap crop = hm_new(128, 128);              /* the site shows at most 128x128 */
    for (int y = 0; y < 128; y++) memcpy(crop.z + y * 128, m.z + y * 129, 128 * sizeof(float));
    hm_write_pgm(&crop);
    hm_free(&crop);
    hm_free(&m);
    return 0;
}

--- hint
`(n & (n - 1)) == 0` is true exactly when `n` is a power of two (for `n > 0`).

--- hint
In the square pass, rows `y` that are multiples of `step` have their midpoints at `x = half, half + step, ...`; the rows in between (odd multiples of `half`) have them at `x = 0, step, 2 * step, ...`.

--- harness
static float ref_ds_r(int x, int y, uint32_t seed, float amp) { return (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp; }
static void ref_ds(float *z, int w, uint32_t seed, float rough) {
    int n = w - 1;
    for (int y = 0; y < w; y += n) for (int x = 0; x < w; x += n) z[y * w + x] = hash01(x, y, seed);
    float amp = 0.5f;
    for (int step = n; step > 1; step /= 2, amp *= rough) {
        int half = step / 2;
        for (int y = half; y < w; y += step) for (int x = half; x < w; x += step)
            z[y * w + x] = (z[(y - half) * w + x - half] + z[(y - half) * w + x + half] + z[(y + half) * w + x - half] + z[(y + half) * w + x + half]) / 4 + ref_ds_r(x, y, seed, amp);
        for (int y = 0; y < w; y += half) for (int x = 0; x < w; x += half) {
            if (((x / half) + (y / half)) % 2 == 0) continue;
            float s = 0; int c = 0;
            if (x - half >= 0) { s += z[y * w + x - half]; c++; }
            if (x + half < w) { s += z[y * w + x + half]; c++; }
            if (y - half >= 0) { s += z[(y - half) * w + x]; c++; }
            if (y + half < w) { s += z[(y + half) * w + x]; c++; }
            z[y * w + x] = s / c + ref_ds_r(x, y, seed, amp);
        }
    }
}
static int ds_close(const float *a, const float *b, int n) {
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) if (fabsf(a[i] - b[i]) > 1e-5f) return 0;
    return 1;
}
int main(void) {
    int sizes[] = {2, 3, 5, 17, 65, 257};
    for (int k = 0; k < 6; k++) {
        int w = sizes[k];
        Heightmap m = hm_new(w, w);
        float *ref = calloc((size_t)w * w, sizeof(float));
        CHECK_INT(hm_diamond_square(&m, 99u + k, 0.5f + 0.05f * k), 1);
        ref_ds(ref, w, 99u + k, 0.5f + 0.05f * k);
        CHECK(ds_close(m.z, ref, w * w));
        free(ref); hm_free(&m);
    }
    Heightmap c = hm_new(3, 3);
    hm_diamond_square(&c, 5, 0.5f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&c, 0, 0) - hash01(0, 0, 5)) < 1e-7f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&c, 2, 2) - hash01(2, 2, 5)) < 1e-7f);
    float center = (hash01(0, 0, 5) + hash01(2, 0, 5) + hash01(0, 2, 5) + hash01(2, 2, 5)) / 4 + (hash01(1, 1, 6) * 2 - 1) * 0.5f;
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&c, 1, 1) - center) < 1e-6f);
    float top = (hash01(0, 0, 5) + hash01(2, 0, 5) + center) / 3 + (hash01(1, 0, 6) * 2 - 1) * 0.5f;
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&c, 1, 0) - top) < 1e-6f);
    hm_free(&c);

    int bad[][2] = {{4, 4}, {6, 6}, {5, 9}, {1, 1}, {10, 10}};
    for (int k = 0; k < 5; k++) {
        Heightmap b = hm_new(bad[k][0], bad[k][1]);
        for (int i = 0; i < b.w * b.h; i++) b.z[i] = 7;
        CHECK_INT(hm_diamond_square(&b, 1, 0.5f), 0);
        int changed = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < b.w * b.h; i++) changed += b.z[i] != 7;
        CHECK_INT(changed, 0);
        hm_free(&b);
    }
    Heightmap big = hm_new(1025, 1025);
    CHECK_INT(hm_diamond_square(&big, 3, 0.6f), 1);
    double sum = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 1025 * 1025; i++) sum += big.z[i];
    CHECK(sum == sum);
    float *ref = malloc(sizeof(float) * 1025 * 1025);
    ref_ds(ref, 1025, 3, 0.6f);
    CHECK(ds_close(big.z, ref, 1025 * 1025));
    free(ref);
    hm_free(&big);
    return TEST_END();
}

=== exercise hm_shape
title: Terrain 3: island mask and terraces
continues: hm_diamond
--- prompt
Two shaping passes, both pure per-point functions.

`void hm_island(Heightmap *m, float inner, float outer)`: for each point compute normalized coordinates `nx = 2.0f * x / (w - 1) - 1` and `ny = 2.0f * y / (h - 1) - 1` (so the map spans -1..1), the distance `d = sqrtf(nx * nx + ny * ny)`, and multiply the height by `1 - smoothstepf(inner, outer, d)`. Inside `inner` the terrain is untouched, beyond `outer` it is 0 (sea floor). `smoothstepf` is in the starter.

`void hm_terrace(Heightmap *m, int levels, float sharpness)`: with `t = z * levels`, `k = floorf(t)`, `f = t - k`, set `z = (k + powf(f, sharpness)) / levels`. `sharpness = 1` changes nothing (up to rounding); larger values make flat steps with steep risers.

Assume `w, h >= 2`.

> **Performance:** compute `ny` once per row, outside the `x` loop, and `2.0f / (w - 1)` once per call. Loop-invariant hoisting is something `-O2` does for you only when it can prove it is safe; doing it yourself also makes the intent clear.

--- stubs
void hm_island(Heightmap *m, float inner, float outer) {
    (void)m; (void)inner; (void)outer;
}

void hm_terrace(Heightmap *m, int levels, float sharpness) {
    (void)m; (void)levels; (void)sharpness;
}

--- solution
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <math.h>

/* ===== From earlier chapters: vectors, hashing, gradient noise, fBm, images ===== */

typedef struct { float x, y, z; } Vec3;
Vec3 v3(float x, float y, float z) { Vec3 r = {x, y, z}; return r; }
Vec3 v3_add(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x + b.x, a.y + b.y, a.z + b.z); }
Vec3 v3_sub(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y, a.z - b.z); }
Vec3 v3_scale(Vec3 a, float s) { return v3(a.x * s, a.y * s, a.z * s); }
float v3_dot(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return a.x * b.x + a.y * b.y + a.z * b.z; }
Vec3 v3_norm(Vec3 a) { float l = sqrtf(v3_dot(a, a)); return l > 0 ? v3_scale(a, 1.0f / l) : a; }

uint32_t hash32(uint32_t x) {            /* lowbias32 (Chris Wellons) */
    x ^= x >> 16; x *= 0x7feb352dU; x ^= x >> 15; x *= 0x846ca68bU; x ^= x >> 16;
    return x;
}
uint32_t hash2(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {
    return hash32((uint32_t)x * 0x27d4eb2dU ^ hash32((uint32_t)y ^ seed));
}
float hash01(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {     /* uniform in [0, 1) */
    return (float)(hash2(x, y, seed) >> 8) * (1.0f / 16777216.0f);
}
float lerpf(float a, float b, float t) { return a + (b - a) * t; }
float fade(float t) { return t * t * t * (t * (t * 6 - 15) + 10); }   /* Perlin 2002 quintic */
float clampf(float v, float lo, float hi) { return v < lo ? lo : v > hi ? hi : v; }
float smoothstepf(float e0, float e1, float v) {
    float t = clampf((v - e0) / (e1 - e0), 0.0f, 1.0f);
    return t * t * (3 - 2 * t);
}

static const float GRAD_X[8] = {1, -1, 0, 0, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
static const float GRAD_Y[8] = {0, 0, 1, -1, 0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
float grad_dot(uint32_t h, float dx, float dy) { return GRAD_X[h & 7] * dx + GRAD_Y[h & 7] * dy; }

/* 2D gradient (Perlin-style) noise, roughly in [-1, 1], 0 at integer lattice points */
float gnoise2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed) {
    float fx0 = floorf(x), fy0 = floorf(y);
    int ix = (int)fx0, iy = (int)fy0;
    float fx = x - fx0, fy = y - fy0;
    float n00 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy, seed), fx, fy);
    float n10 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy, seed), fx - 1, fy);
    float n01 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy + 1, seed), fx, fy - 1);
    float n11 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy + 1, seed), fx - 1, fy - 1);
    float u = fade(fx), v = fade(fy);
    return 1.41421356f * lerpf(lerpf(n00, n10, u), lerpf(n01, n11, u), v);
}

/* fractal Brownian motion: octaves of noise, frequency x lacunarity, amplitude x gain; result ~[-1, 1] */
float fbm2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed, int octaves, float lacunarity, float gain) {
    float sum = 0, amp = 1, norm = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
        sum += amp * gnoise2(x, y, seed + (uint32_t)i * 7919u);
        norm += amp;
        x *= lacunarity; y *= lacunarity; amp *= gain;
    }
    return norm > 0 ? sum / norm : 0;
}

typedef struct { int w, h; unsigned char *px; } Image;      /* RGB, 3 bytes per pixel, row-major */
Image img_new(int w, int h) { Image im = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h * 3, 1)}; return im; }
void img_free(Image *im) { free(im->px); im->px = NULL; }
void img_set(Image *im, int x, int y, int r, int g, int b) {
    unsigned char *p = im->px + ((size_t)y * im->w + x) * 3;
    p[0] = (unsigned char)r; p[1] = (unsigned char)g; p[2] = (unsigned char)b;
}
void img_write_ppm(const Image *im) {                       /* ASCII P3 to stdout: the site draws it */
    printf("P3\n%d %d\n255\n", im->w, im->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < im->h; y++) {
        const unsigned char *p = im->px + (size_t)y * im->w * 3;
        for (int x = 0; x < im->w * 3; x++) printf("%d%c", p[x], x + 1 < im->w * 3 ? ' ' : '\n');
    }
}

/* ===== This chapter: the heightmap, a flat row-major float array ===== */

typedef struct { int w, h; float *z; } Heightmap;           /* height of (x, y) is z[y * w + x] */
Heightmap hm_new(int w, int h) { Heightmap m = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h, sizeof(float))}; return m; }
void hm_free(Heightmap *m) { free(m->z); m->z = NULL; }
float hm_get(const Heightmap *m, int x, int y) { return m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x]; }
void hm_write_pgm(const Heightmap *m) {                     /* heights in [0, 1] as a P2 image */
    printf("P2\n%d %d\n255\n", m->w, m->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float v = clampf(hm_get(m, x, y), 0, 1);
            printf("%d%c", (int)(v * 255 + 0.5f), x + 1 < m->w ? ' ' : '\n');
        }
}

void hm_fbm(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float scale, int octaves) {
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++)
            m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x] = fbm2(x * scale, y * scale, seed, octaves, 2.0f, 0.5f);
}

void hm_minmax(const Heightmap *m, float *lo, float *hi) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float a = m->z[0], b = m->z[0];
    for (size_t i = 1; i < n; i++) {
        if (m->z[i] < a) a = m->z[i];
        if (m->z[i] > b) b = m->z[i];
    }
    *lo = a; *hi = b;
}

void hm_normalize(Heightmap *m) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float lo, hi;
    hm_minmax(m, &lo, &hi);
    if (!(hi > lo)) {
        memset(m->z, 0, n * sizeof(float));
        return;
    }
    float s = 1.0f / (hi - lo);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) m->z[i] = (m->z[i] - lo) * s;
}

int hm_diamond_square(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float roughness) {
    int n = m->w - 1, w = m->w;
    if (m->w != m->h || n < 1 || (n & (n - 1)) != 0) return 0;
    float *z = m->z;
    z[0] = hash01(0, 0, seed);
    z[n] = hash01(n, 0, seed);
    z[(size_t)n * w] = hash01(0, n, seed);
    z[(size_t)n * w + n] = hash01(n, n, seed);
    float amp = 0.5f;
    for (int step = n; step > 1; step /= 2, amp *= roughness) {
        int half = step / 2;
        for (int y = half; y < w; y += step)          /* diamond: square centers */
            for (int x = half; x < w; x += step) {
                float avg = (z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x - half] + z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x + half] +
                             z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x - half] + z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x + half]) * 0.25f;
                z[(size_t)y * w + x] = avg + (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp;
            }
        for (int y = 0; y < w; y += half)             /* square: edge midpoints */
            for (int x = (y / half) % 2 == 0 ? half : 0; x < w; x += step) {
                float sum = 0;
                int cnt = 0;
                if (x >= half) { sum += z[(size_t)y * w + x - half]; cnt++; }
                if (x + half < w) { sum += z[(size_t)y * w + x + half]; cnt++; }
                if (y >= half) { sum += z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x]; cnt++; }
                if (y + half < w) { sum += z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x]; cnt++; }
                z[(size_t)y * w + x] = sum / cnt + (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp;
            }
    }
    return 1;
}

void hm_island(Heightmap *m, float inner, float outer) {
    float sx = 2.0f / (m->w - 1), sy = 2.0f / (m->h - 1);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++) {
        float ny = y * sy - 1;
        float *row = m->z + (size_t)y * m->w;
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float nx = x * sx - 1;
            float d = sqrtf(nx * nx + ny * ny);
            row[x] *= 1 - smoothstepf(inner, outer, d);
        }
    }
}

void hm_terrace(Heightmap *m, int levels, float sharpness) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        float t = m->z[i] * levels, k = floorf(t), f = t - k;
        m->z[i] = (k + powf(f, sharpness)) / levels;
    }
}

int main(void) {
    Heightmap m = hm_new(128, 128);
    hm_fbm(&m, 7, 1.0f / 40, 6);
    hm_normalize(&m);
    hm_island(&m, 0.3f, 0.95f);
    hm_terrace(&m, 8, 3.0f);
    hm_write_pgm(&m);
    hm_free(&m);
    return 0;
}

--- hint
`2.0f * x / (w - 1) - 1` and `x * (2.0f / (w - 1)) - 1` differ only in the last bit; the tests allow for that.

--- harness
int main(void) {
    Heightmap m = hm_new(41, 21);
    for (int i = 0; i < 41 * 21; i++) m.z[i] = 1;
    hm_island(&m, 0.2f, 0.9f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 20, 10) - 1) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 0, 0)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 40, 20)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 0, 10)) < 1e-6f);
    float nx = 2.0f * 28 / 40 - 1, ny = 2.0f * 13 / 20 - 1, d = sqrtf(nx * nx + ny * ny);
    float t = (d - 0.2f) / 0.7f;
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 28, 13) - (1 - t * t * (3 - 2 * t))) < 1e-5f);
    nx = 2.0f * 10 / 40 - 1; ny = 2.0f * 3 / 20 - 1; d = sqrtf(nx * nx + ny * ny); t = (d - 0.2f) / 0.7f;
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 10, 3) - (1 - t * t * (3 - 2 * t))) < 1e-5f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 20, 0) - hm_get(&m, 20, 20)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&m, 5, 10) - hm_get(&m, 35, 10)) < 1e-6f);
    hm_free(&m);

    Heightmap v = hm_new(5, 5);
    for (int i = 0; i < 25; i++) v.z[i] = 0.5f;
    hm_island(&v, 0.0f, 2.0f);
    t = 0.5f;
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&v, 2, 2) - 0.5f) < 1e-6f);
    d = sqrtf(2.0f); t = d / 2;
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&v, 4, 4) - 0.5f * (1 - t * t * (3 - 2 * t))) < 1e-5f);
    hm_free(&v);

    Heightmap tr = hm_new(4, 2);
    float in[8] = {0.0f, 0.1f, 0.26f, 0.5f, 0.74f, 0.99f, 1.0f, 0.625f};
    memcpy(tr.z, in, sizeof in);
    hm_terrace(&tr, 4, 2.0f);
    for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++) {
        float tt = in[i] * 4, k = floorf(tt), f = tt - k;
        CHECK(fabsf(tr.z[i] - (k + f * f) / 4) < 1e-5f);
    }
    memcpy(tr.z, in, sizeof in);
    hm_terrace(&tr, 7, 1.0f);
    for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++) CHECK(fabsf(tr.z[i] - in[i]) < 1e-5f);
    hm_free(&tr);

    Heightmap mono = hm_new(100, 2);
    for (int i = 0; i < 200; i++) mono.z[i] = (i % 100) / 99.0f;
    hm_terrace(&mono, 5, 4.0f);
    int ok = 1;
    for (int i = 1; i < 100; i++) if (mono.z[i] < mono.z[i - 1] - 1e-6f) ok = 0;
    CHECK(ok);
    CHECK(fabsf(mono.z[99] - 1) < 1e-5f);
    CHECK(fabsf(mono.z[25] - (1 + powf(25 / 99.0f * 5 - 1, 4)) / 5) < 1e-5f);
    hm_free(&mono);
    return TEST_END();
}

=== exercise hm_thermal
title: Terrain 4: thermal erosion
continues: hm_shape
--- prompt
Implement `void hm_thermal(Heightmap *m, int iterations, float talus, float rate)`.

Each iteration is one pass over all cells, reading the heights as they were **at the start of the pass**:

- for cell `c` with height `h`, look at its 4 neighbors (left, right, up, down) that are inside the map; `d_i = h - h_i`;
- `d_max` = the largest `d_i`; `d_total` = the sum of the `d_i` that are `> talus`;
- if `d_max > talus`: `amount = rate * (d_max - talus)`, and for each neighbor with `d_i > talus` move `amount * d_i / d_total` from `c` to that neighbor.

Accumulate all moves in a separate delta buffer, and add the deltas to the heights after the pass. Material is only moved, so the total height is conserved (up to float rounding).

The two classic mistakes:

- **in-place updates**: if you add to the neighbor's height immediately, a cell visited later in the same pass sees the already-moved material, it can be passed along again, and erosion streaks in the scan direction;
- **allocating the delta buffer inside the iteration loop**: allocate it once per call, `memset` it to zero per iteration, free it at the end.

Use `talus >= 0` and `rate <= 0.5` for stability (the tests do).

--- stubs
void hm_thermal(Heightmap *m, int iterations, float talus, float rate) {
    (void)m; (void)iterations; (void)talus; (void)rate;
}

--- solution
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <math.h>

/* ===== From earlier chapters: vectors, hashing, gradient noise, fBm, images ===== */

typedef struct { float x, y, z; } Vec3;
Vec3 v3(float x, float y, float z) { Vec3 r = {x, y, z}; return r; }
Vec3 v3_add(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x + b.x, a.y + b.y, a.z + b.z); }
Vec3 v3_sub(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y, a.z - b.z); }
Vec3 v3_scale(Vec3 a, float s) { return v3(a.x * s, a.y * s, a.z * s); }
float v3_dot(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return a.x * b.x + a.y * b.y + a.z * b.z; }
Vec3 v3_norm(Vec3 a) { float l = sqrtf(v3_dot(a, a)); return l > 0 ? v3_scale(a, 1.0f / l) : a; }

uint32_t hash32(uint32_t x) {            /* lowbias32 (Chris Wellons) */
    x ^= x >> 16; x *= 0x7feb352dU; x ^= x >> 15; x *= 0x846ca68bU; x ^= x >> 16;
    return x;
}
uint32_t hash2(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {
    return hash32((uint32_t)x * 0x27d4eb2dU ^ hash32((uint32_t)y ^ seed));
}
float hash01(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {     /* uniform in [0, 1) */
    return (float)(hash2(x, y, seed) >> 8) * (1.0f / 16777216.0f);
}
float lerpf(float a, float b, float t) { return a + (b - a) * t; }
float fade(float t) { return t * t * t * (t * (t * 6 - 15) + 10); }   /* Perlin 2002 quintic */
float clampf(float v, float lo, float hi) { return v < lo ? lo : v > hi ? hi : v; }
float smoothstepf(float e0, float e1, float v) {
    float t = clampf((v - e0) / (e1 - e0), 0.0f, 1.0f);
    return t * t * (3 - 2 * t);
}

static const float GRAD_X[8] = {1, -1, 0, 0, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
static const float GRAD_Y[8] = {0, 0, 1, -1, 0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
float grad_dot(uint32_t h, float dx, float dy) { return GRAD_X[h & 7] * dx + GRAD_Y[h & 7] * dy; }

/* 2D gradient (Perlin-style) noise, roughly in [-1, 1], 0 at integer lattice points */
float gnoise2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed) {
    float fx0 = floorf(x), fy0 = floorf(y);
    int ix = (int)fx0, iy = (int)fy0;
    float fx = x - fx0, fy = y - fy0;
    float n00 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy, seed), fx, fy);
    float n10 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy, seed), fx - 1, fy);
    float n01 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy + 1, seed), fx, fy - 1);
    float n11 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy + 1, seed), fx - 1, fy - 1);
    float u = fade(fx), v = fade(fy);
    return 1.41421356f * lerpf(lerpf(n00, n10, u), lerpf(n01, n11, u), v);
}

/* fractal Brownian motion: octaves of noise, frequency x lacunarity, amplitude x gain; result ~[-1, 1] */
float fbm2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed, int octaves, float lacunarity, float gain) {
    float sum = 0, amp = 1, norm = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
        sum += amp * gnoise2(x, y, seed + (uint32_t)i * 7919u);
        norm += amp;
        x *= lacunarity; y *= lacunarity; amp *= gain;
    }
    return norm > 0 ? sum / norm : 0;
}

typedef struct { int w, h; unsigned char *px; } Image;      /* RGB, 3 bytes per pixel, row-major */
Image img_new(int w, int h) { Image im = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h * 3, 1)}; return im; }
void img_free(Image *im) { free(im->px); im->px = NULL; }
void img_set(Image *im, int x, int y, int r, int g, int b) {
    unsigned char *p = im->px + ((size_t)y * im->w + x) * 3;
    p[0] = (unsigned char)r; p[1] = (unsigned char)g; p[2] = (unsigned char)b;
}
void img_write_ppm(const Image *im) {                       /* ASCII P3 to stdout: the site draws it */
    printf("P3\n%d %d\n255\n", im->w, im->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < im->h; y++) {
        const unsigned char *p = im->px + (size_t)y * im->w * 3;
        for (int x = 0; x < im->w * 3; x++) printf("%d%c", p[x], x + 1 < im->w * 3 ? ' ' : '\n');
    }
}

/* ===== This chapter: the heightmap, a flat row-major float array ===== */

typedef struct { int w, h; float *z; } Heightmap;           /* height of (x, y) is z[y * w + x] */
Heightmap hm_new(int w, int h) { Heightmap m = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h, sizeof(float))}; return m; }
void hm_free(Heightmap *m) { free(m->z); m->z = NULL; }
float hm_get(const Heightmap *m, int x, int y) { return m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x]; }
void hm_write_pgm(const Heightmap *m) {                     /* heights in [0, 1] as a P2 image */
    printf("P2\n%d %d\n255\n", m->w, m->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float v = clampf(hm_get(m, x, y), 0, 1);
            printf("%d%c", (int)(v * 255 + 0.5f), x + 1 < m->w ? ' ' : '\n');
        }
}

void hm_fbm(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float scale, int octaves) {
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++)
            m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x] = fbm2(x * scale, y * scale, seed, octaves, 2.0f, 0.5f);
}

void hm_minmax(const Heightmap *m, float *lo, float *hi) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float a = m->z[0], b = m->z[0];
    for (size_t i = 1; i < n; i++) {
        if (m->z[i] < a) a = m->z[i];
        if (m->z[i] > b) b = m->z[i];
    }
    *lo = a; *hi = b;
}

void hm_normalize(Heightmap *m) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float lo, hi;
    hm_minmax(m, &lo, &hi);
    if (!(hi > lo)) {
        memset(m->z, 0, n * sizeof(float));
        return;
    }
    float s = 1.0f / (hi - lo);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) m->z[i] = (m->z[i] - lo) * s;
}

int hm_diamond_square(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float roughness) {
    int n = m->w - 1, w = m->w;
    if (m->w != m->h || n < 1 || (n & (n - 1)) != 0) return 0;
    float *z = m->z;
    z[0] = hash01(0, 0, seed);
    z[n] = hash01(n, 0, seed);
    z[(size_t)n * w] = hash01(0, n, seed);
    z[(size_t)n * w + n] = hash01(n, n, seed);
    float amp = 0.5f;
    for (int step = n; step > 1; step /= 2, amp *= roughness) {
        int half = step / 2;
        for (int y = half; y < w; y += step)          /* diamond: square centers */
            for (int x = half; x < w; x += step) {
                float avg = (z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x - half] + z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x + half] +
                             z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x - half] + z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x + half]) * 0.25f;
                z[(size_t)y * w + x] = avg + (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp;
            }
        for (int y = 0; y < w; y += half)             /* square: edge midpoints */
            for (int x = (y / half) % 2 == 0 ? half : 0; x < w; x += step) {
                float sum = 0;
                int cnt = 0;
                if (x >= half) { sum += z[(size_t)y * w + x - half]; cnt++; }
                if (x + half < w) { sum += z[(size_t)y * w + x + half]; cnt++; }
                if (y >= half) { sum += z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x]; cnt++; }
                if (y + half < w) { sum += z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x]; cnt++; }
                z[(size_t)y * w + x] = sum / cnt + (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp;
            }
    }
    return 1;
}

void hm_island(Heightmap *m, float inner, float outer) {
    float sx = 2.0f / (m->w - 1), sy = 2.0f / (m->h - 1);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++) {
        float ny = y * sy - 1;
        float *row = m->z + (size_t)y * m->w;
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float nx = x * sx - 1;
            float d = sqrtf(nx * nx + ny * ny);
            row[x] *= 1 - smoothstepf(inner, outer, d);
        }
    }
}

void hm_terrace(Heightmap *m, int levels, float sharpness) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        float t = m->z[i] * levels, k = floorf(t), f = t - k;
        m->z[i] = (k + powf(f, sharpness)) / levels;
    }
}

void hm_thermal(Heightmap *m, int iterations, float talus, float rate) {
    static const int DX[4] = {-1, 1, 0, 0}, DY[4] = {0, 0, -1, 1};
    int w = m->w, h = m->h;
    size_t n = (size_t)w * h;
    float *delta = malloc(n * sizeof(float));
    for (int it = 0; it < iterations; it++) {
        memset(delta, 0, n * sizeof(float));
        for (int y = 0; y < h; y++)
            for (int x = 0; x < w; x++) {
                size_t c = (size_t)y * w + x;
                float hc = m->z[c], d[4] = {0, 0, 0, 0}, dmax = 0, dtot = 0;
                for (int k = 0; k < 4; k++) {
                    int nx = x + DX[k], ny = y + DY[k];
                    if (nx < 0 || ny < 0 || nx >= w || ny >= h) continue;
                    d[k] = hc - m->z[(size_t)ny * w + nx];
                    if (d[k] > dmax) dmax = d[k];
                    if (d[k] > talus) dtot += d[k];
                }
                if (dmax <= talus) continue;
                float amount = rate * (dmax - talus);
                for (int k = 0; k < 4; k++) {
                    if (!(d[k] > talus)) continue;
                    float s = amount * d[k] / dtot;
                    delta[(size_t)(y + DY[k]) * w + x + DX[k]] += s;
                    delta[c] -= s;
                }
            }
        for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) m->z[i] += delta[i];
    }
    free(delta);
}

int main(void) {
    Heightmap m = hm_new(128, 128);
    hm_fbm(&m, 7, 1.0f / 40, 6);
    hm_normalize(&m);
    hm_island(&m, 0.3f, 0.95f);
    hm_terrace(&m, 6, 4.0f);
    hm_thermal(&m, 30, 0.004f, 0.4f);    /* softens the terrace risers into talus slopes */
    hm_write_pgm(&m);
    hm_free(&m);
    return 0;
}

--- hint
Out-of-map neighbors simply do not take part: leave their `d_i` at 0, which is never `> talus` for `talus >= 0`.

--- hint
`d[k] = 0` for a missing neighbor also keeps it from raising `d_max` above 0, which is what you want.

--- harness
static void ref_thermal(float *z, int w, int h, int iters, float talus, float rate) {
    float *d = calloc((size_t)w * h, sizeof(float));
    for (int it = 0; it < iters; it++) {
        memset(d, 0, sizeof(float) * w * h);
        for (int y = 0; y < h; y++) for (int x = 0; x < w; x++) {
            int nb[4][2] = {{x - 1, y}, {x + 1, y}, {x, y - 1}, {x, y + 1}};
            float di[4] = {0}, mx = 0, tot = 0, hc = z[y * w + x];
            for (int k = 0; k < 4; k++) {
                if (nb[k][0] < 0 || nb[k][1] < 0 || nb[k][0] >= w || nb[k][1] >= h) continue;
                di[k] = hc - z[nb[k][1] * w + nb[k][0]];
                mx = fmaxf(mx, di[k]);
                if (di[k] > talus) tot += di[k];
            }
            if (mx <= talus) continue;
            for (int k = 0; k < 4; k++) if (di[k] > talus) {
                float s = rate * (mx - talus) * di[k] / tot;
                d[nb[k][1] * w + nb[k][0]] += s; d[y * w + x] -= s;
            }
        }
        for (int i = 0; i < w * h; i++) z[i] += d[i];
    }
    free(d);
}
int main(void) {
    /* a single spike spreads symmetrically to its 4 neighbors */
    Heightmap s = hm_new(5, 5);
    s.z[2 * 5 + 2] = 1;
    hm_thermal(&s, 1, 0.1f, 0.4f);
    float moved = 0.4f * 0.9f / 4;
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&s, 2, 2) - (1 - 0.4f * 0.9f)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&s, 1, 2) - moved) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&s, 3, 2) - moved) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&s, 2, 1) - moved) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&s, 2, 3) - moved) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(hm_get(&s, 1, 1) == 0);
    hm_free(&s);

    /* a step: only the lower side receives, proportionally; edge cells have fewer neighbors */
    Heightmap st = hm_new(3, 1);
    st.z[0] = 1; st.z[1] = 0.5f; st.z[2] = 0;
    hm_thermal(&st, 1, 0.2f, 0.5f);
    CHECK(fabsf(st.z[0] - (1 - 0.15f)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(st.z[1] - (0.5f + 0.15f - 0.15f)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(st.z[2] - 0.15f) < 1e-6f);
    hm_free(&st);

    /* uneven drops: proportional split, and in-place updates would give a different answer */
    Heightmap u = hm_new(3, 3);
    float init[9] = {0.2f, 0.9f, 0.3f, 0.5f, 1.0f, 0.0f, 0.8f, 0.6f, 0.4f};
    memcpy(u.z, init, sizeof init);
    float ref9[9]; memcpy(ref9, init, sizeof init);
    hm_thermal(&u, 1, 0.05f, 0.3f);
    ref_thermal(ref9, 3, 3, 1, 0.05f, 0.3f);
    int ok = 1;
    for (int i = 0; i < 9; i++) if (fabsf(u.z[i] - ref9[i]) > 1e-6f) ok = 0;
    CHECK(ok);
    hm_free(&u);

    /* nothing steeper than talus: no change */
    Heightmap f = hm_new(16, 16);
    for (int y = 0; y < 16; y++) for (int x = 0; x < 16; x++) f.z[y * 16 + x] = x * 0.01f;
    hm_thermal(&f, 5, 0.02f, 0.5f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_get(&f, 9, 4) - 0.09f) < 1e-7f);
    hm_free(&f);

    /* real terrain: matches the reference, conserves mass, flattens the steepest slope */
    Heightmap t = hm_new(96, 80);
    hm_fbm(&t, 11, 0.08f, 5);
    hm_normalize(&t);
    float *r = malloc(sizeof(float) * 96 * 80);
    memcpy(r, t.z, sizeof(float) * 96 * 80);
    double before = 0, after = 0;
    float steep0 = 0, steep1 = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 96 * 80; i++) { before += t.z[i]; if (i % 96) steep0 = fmaxf(steep0, fabsf(t.z[i] - t.z[i - 1])); }
    hm_thermal(&t, 25, 0.01f, 0.25f);
    ref_thermal(r, 96, 80, 25, 0.01f, 0.25f);
    float maxdiff = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 96 * 80; i++) { after += t.z[i]; maxdiff = fmaxf(maxdiff, fabsf(t.z[i] - r[i])); if (i % 96) steep1 = fmaxf(steep1, fabsf(t.z[i] - t.z[i - 1])); }
    CHECK(maxdiff < 1e-4f);
    CHECK(fabs(after - before) < 1e-2);
    CHECK(steep1 < steep0);
    free(r);
    hm_free(&t);

    Heightmap big = hm_new(512, 512);
    hm_fbm(&big, 3, 0.02f, 6);
    hm_thermal(&big, 20, 0.002f, 0.4f);
    CHECK(big.z[1000] == big.z[1000]);
    hm_free(&big);
    return TEST_END();
}

=== exercise hm_sample
title: Terrain 5: bilinear height and gradient
continues: hm_thermal
--- prompt
A water droplet lives *between* grid points, so it needs the height and the slope at fractional positions. Implement

```c
float hm_sample(const Heightmap *m, float x, float y, float *gx, float *gy);
```

With `ix = (int)x`, `iy = (int)y`, `fx = x - ix`, `fy = y - iy`, and the four corner heights `nw = (ix, iy)`, `ne = (ix + 1, iy)`, `sw = (ix, iy + 1)`, `se = (ix + 1, iy + 1)`:

```text
height = nw (1-fx)(1-fy) + ne fx (1-fy) + sw (1-fx) fy + se fx fy
gx     = (ne - nw)(1 - fy) + (se - sw) fy        (d height / dx)
gy     = (sw - nw)(1 - fx) + (se - ne) fx        (d height / dy)
```

This is exactly the derivative of the bilinear patch, the same formulas Lague uses. `gx` and `gy` may be `NULL` (caller only wants the height). The caller guarantees `0 <= x < w - 1` and `0 <= y < h - 1`, so all four corners exist; `(int)x` is `floor` for those.

> **Performance:** this is called twice per droplet step, millions of times. Compute the row offset `iy * w` once, read the four values into locals, and do not call a "get with bounds check" helper four times.

--- stubs
float hm_sample(const Heightmap *m, float x, float y, float *gx, float *gy) {
    (void)m; (void)x; (void)y;
    if (gx) *gx = 0;
    if (gy) *gy = 0;
    return 0;
}

--- solution
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <math.h>

/* ===== From earlier chapters: vectors, hashing, gradient noise, fBm, images ===== */

typedef struct { float x, y, z; } Vec3;
Vec3 v3(float x, float y, float z) { Vec3 r = {x, y, z}; return r; }
Vec3 v3_add(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x + b.x, a.y + b.y, a.z + b.z); }
Vec3 v3_sub(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return v3(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y, a.z - b.z); }
Vec3 v3_scale(Vec3 a, float s) { return v3(a.x * s, a.y * s, a.z * s); }
float v3_dot(Vec3 a, Vec3 b) { return a.x * b.x + a.y * b.y + a.z * b.z; }
Vec3 v3_norm(Vec3 a) { float l = sqrtf(v3_dot(a, a)); return l > 0 ? v3_scale(a, 1.0f / l) : a; }

uint32_t hash32(uint32_t x) {            /* lowbias32 (Chris Wellons) */
    x ^= x >> 16; x *= 0x7feb352dU; x ^= x >> 15; x *= 0x846ca68bU; x ^= x >> 16;
    return x;
}
uint32_t hash2(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {
    return hash32((uint32_t)x * 0x27d4eb2dU ^ hash32((uint32_t)y ^ seed));
}
float hash01(int x, int y, uint32_t seed) {     /* uniform in [0, 1) */
    return (float)(hash2(x, y, seed) >> 8) * (1.0f / 16777216.0f);
}
float lerpf(float a, float b, float t) { return a + (b - a) * t; }
float fade(float t) { return t * t * t * (t * (t * 6 - 15) + 10); }   /* Perlin 2002 quintic */
float clampf(float v, float lo, float hi) { return v < lo ? lo : v > hi ? hi : v; }
float smoothstepf(float e0, float e1, float v) {
    float t = clampf((v - e0) / (e1 - e0), 0.0f, 1.0f);
    return t * t * (3 - 2 * t);
}

static const float GRAD_X[8] = {1, -1, 0, 0, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
static const float GRAD_Y[8] = {0, 0, 1, -1, 0.70710678f, 0.70710678f, -0.70710678f, -0.70710678f};
float grad_dot(uint32_t h, float dx, float dy) { return GRAD_X[h & 7] * dx + GRAD_Y[h & 7] * dy; }

/* 2D gradient (Perlin-style) noise, roughly in [-1, 1], 0 at integer lattice points */
float gnoise2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed) {
    float fx0 = floorf(x), fy0 = floorf(y);
    int ix = (int)fx0, iy = (int)fy0;
    float fx = x - fx0, fy = y - fy0;
    float n00 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy, seed), fx, fy);
    float n10 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy, seed), fx - 1, fy);
    float n01 = grad_dot(hash2(ix, iy + 1, seed), fx, fy - 1);
    float n11 = grad_dot(hash2(ix + 1, iy + 1, seed), fx - 1, fy - 1);
    float u = fade(fx), v = fade(fy);
    return 1.41421356f * lerpf(lerpf(n00, n10, u), lerpf(n01, n11, u), v);
}

/* fractal Brownian motion: octaves of noise, frequency x lacunarity, amplitude x gain; result ~[-1, 1] */
float fbm2(float x, float y, uint32_t seed, int octaves, float lacunarity, float gain) {
    float sum = 0, amp = 1, norm = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
        sum += amp * gnoise2(x, y, seed + (uint32_t)i * 7919u);
        norm += amp;
        x *= lacunarity; y *= lacunarity; amp *= gain;
    }
    return norm > 0 ? sum / norm : 0;
}

typedef struct { int w, h; unsigned char *px; } Image;      /* RGB, 3 bytes per pixel, row-major */
Image img_new(int w, int h) { Image im = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h * 3, 1)}; return im; }
void img_free(Image *im) { free(im->px); im->px = NULL; }
void img_set(Image *im, int x, int y, int r, int g, int b) {
    unsigned char *p = im->px + ((size_t)y * im->w + x) * 3;
    p[0] = (unsigned char)r; p[1] = (unsigned char)g; p[2] = (unsigned char)b;
}
void img_write_ppm(const Image *im) {                       /* ASCII P3 to stdout: the site draws it */
    printf("P3\n%d %d\n255\n", im->w, im->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < im->h; y++) {
        const unsigned char *p = im->px + (size_t)y * im->w * 3;
        for (int x = 0; x < im->w * 3; x++) printf("%d%c", p[x], x + 1 < im->w * 3 ? ' ' : '\n');
    }
}

/* ===== This chapter: the heightmap, a flat row-major float array ===== */

typedef struct { int w, h; float *z; } Heightmap;           /* height of (x, y) is z[y * w + x] */
Heightmap hm_new(int w, int h) { Heightmap m = {w, h, calloc((size_t)w * h, sizeof(float))}; return m; }
void hm_free(Heightmap *m) { free(m->z); m->z = NULL; }
float hm_get(const Heightmap *m, int x, int y) { return m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x]; }
void hm_write_pgm(const Heightmap *m) {                     /* heights in [0, 1] as a P2 image */
    printf("P2\n%d %d\n255\n", m->w, m->h);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float v = clampf(hm_get(m, x, y), 0, 1);
            printf("%d%c", (int)(v * 255 + 0.5f), x + 1 < m->w ? ' ' : '\n');
        }
}

void hm_fbm(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float scale, int octaves) {
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++)
            m->z[(size_t)y * m->w + x] = fbm2(x * scale, y * scale, seed, octaves, 2.0f, 0.5f);
}

void hm_minmax(const Heightmap *m, float *lo, float *hi) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float a = m->z[0], b = m->z[0];
    for (size_t i = 1; i < n; i++) {
        if (m->z[i] < a) a = m->z[i];
        if (m->z[i] > b) b = m->z[i];
    }
    *lo = a; *hi = b;
}

void hm_normalize(Heightmap *m) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    float lo, hi;
    hm_minmax(m, &lo, &hi);
    if (!(hi > lo)) {
        memset(m->z, 0, n * sizeof(float));
        return;
    }
    float s = 1.0f / (hi - lo);
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) m->z[i] = (m->z[i] - lo) * s;
}

int hm_diamond_square(Heightmap *m, uint32_t seed, float roughness) {
    int n = m->w - 1, w = m->w;
    if (m->w != m->h || n < 1 || (n & (n - 1)) != 0) return 0;
    float *z = m->z;
    z[0] = hash01(0, 0, seed);
    z[n] = hash01(n, 0, seed);
    z[(size_t)n * w] = hash01(0, n, seed);
    z[(size_t)n * w + n] = hash01(n, n, seed);
    float amp = 0.5f;
    for (int step = n; step > 1; step /= 2, amp *= roughness) {
        int half = step / 2;
        for (int y = half; y < w; y += step)          /* diamond: square centers */
            for (int x = half; x < w; x += step) {
                float avg = (z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x - half] + z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x + half] +
                             z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x - half] + z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x + half]) * 0.25f;
                z[(size_t)y * w + x] = avg + (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp;
            }
        for (int y = 0; y < w; y += half)             /* square: edge midpoints */
            for (int x = (y / half) % 2 == 0 ? half : 0; x < w; x += step) {
                float sum = 0;
                int cnt = 0;
                if (x >= half) { sum += z[(size_t)y * w + x - half]; cnt++; }
                if (x + half < w) { sum += z[(size_t)y * w + x + half]; cnt++; }
                if (y >= half) { sum += z[(size_t)(y - half) * w + x]; cnt++; }
                if (y + half < w) { sum += z[(size_t)(y + half) * w + x]; cnt++; }
                z[(size_t)y * w + x] = sum / cnt + (hash01(x, y, seed + 1) * 2 - 1) * amp;
            }
    }
    return 1;
}

void hm_island(Heightmap *m, float inner, float outer) {
    float sx = 2.0f / (m->w - 1), sy = 2.0f / (m->h - 1);
    for (int y = 0; y < m->h; y++) {
        float ny = y * sy - 1;
        float *row = m->z + (size_t)y * m->w;
        for (int x = 0; x < m->w; x++) {
            float nx = x * sx - 1;
            float d = sqrtf(nx * nx + ny * ny);
            row[x] *= 1 - smoothstepf(inner, outer, d);
        }
    }
}

void hm_terrace(Heightmap *m, int levels, float sharpness) {
    size_t n = (size_t)m->w * m->h;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        float t = m->z[i] * levels, k = floorf(t), f = t - k;
        m->z[i] = (k + powf(f, sharpness)) / levels;
    }
}

void hm_thermal(Heightmap *m, int iterations, float talus, float rate) {
    static const int DX[4] = {-1, 1, 0, 0}, DY[4] = {0, 0, -1, 1};
    int w = m->w, h = m->h;
    size_t n = (size_t)w * h;
    float *delta = malloc(n * sizeof(float));
    for (int it = 0; it < iterations; it++) {
        memset(delta, 0, n * sizeof(float));
        for (int y = 0; y < h; y++)
            for (int x = 0; x < w; x++) {
                size_t c = (size_t)y * w + x;
                float hc = m->z[c], d[4] = {0, 0, 0, 0}, dmax = 0, dtot = 0;
                for (int k = 0; k < 4; k++) {
                    int nx = x + DX[k], ny = y + DY[k];
                    if (nx < 0 || ny < 0 || nx >= w || ny >= h) continue;
                    d[k] = hc - m->z[(size_t)ny * w + nx];
                    if (d[k] > dmax) dmax = d[k];
                    if (d[k] > talus) dtot += d[k];
                }
                if (dmax <= talus) continue;
                float amount = rate * (dmax - talus);
                for (int k = 0; k < 4; k++) {
                    if (!(d[k] > talus)) continue;
                    float s = amount * d[k] / dtot;
                    delta[(size_t)(y + DY[k]) * w + x + DX[k]] += s;
                    delta[c] -= s;
                }
            }
        for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) m->z[i] += delta[i];
    }
    free(delta);
}

float hm_sample(const Heightmap *m, float x, float y, float *gx, float *gy) {
    int ix = (int)x, iy = (int)y;
    float fx = x - ix, fy = y - iy;
    const float *p = m->z + (size_t)iy * m->w + ix;
    float nw = p[0], ne = p[1], sw = p[m->w], se = p[m->w + 1];
    if (gx) *gx = (ne - nw) * (1 - fy) + (se - sw) * fy;
    if (gy) *gy = (sw - nw) * (1 - fx) + (se - ne) * fx;
    return nw * (1 - fx) * (1 - fy) + ne * fx * (1 - fy) + sw * (1 - fx) * fy + se * fx * fy;
}

int main(void) {
    /* shade by the sampled slope of a smooth 32x32 map stretched to 128x128 */
    Heightmap m = hm_new(32, 32);
    hm_fbm(&m, 3, 0.15f, 3);
    hm_normalize(&m);
    printf("P2\n128 128\n255\n");
    for (int y = 0; y < 128; y++)
        for (int x = 0; x < 128; x++) {
            float gx, gy, h = hm_sample(&m, x * 31.0f / 128, y * 31.0f / 128, &gx, &gy);
            float lit = clampf(0.5f + (-gx - gy) * 2, 0, 1);
            printf("%d%c", (int)(255 * (0.3f * h + 0.7f * lit)), x == 127 ? '\n' : ' ');
        }
    hm_free(&m);
    return 0;
}

--- hint
Index the four corners from one base pointer: `p[0]`, `p[1]`, `p[w]`, `p[w + 1]`.

--- harness
int main(void) {
    Heightmap m = hm_new(3, 3);
    float v[9] = {0, 1, 2,
                  3, 4, 6,
                  2, 2, 9};
    memcpy(m.z, v, sizeof v);
    float gx, gy;
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_sample(&m, 0, 0, &gx, &gy) - 0) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(gx - 1) < 1e-6f && fabsf(gy - 3) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_sample(&m, 1, 1, &gx, &gy) - 4) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(gx - 2) < 1e-6f && fabsf(gy - (-2)) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(hm_sample(&m, 0.5f, 0.5f, &gx, &gy) - 2) < 1e-6f);
    CHECK(fabsf(gx - 1) < 1e-6f && fabsf(gy - 3) < 1e-6f);
